# Ground Truth Investigation Report

## Verification Status

This file replaces the prior stub `ground_truth.md`. The previous Codex run produced a broad set of structured artifacts, but the Markdown ground truth itself contained only a short scope note. I verified the controlling prompt, the artifact inventory, the source-file inventory, and the final validation notes before synthesizing this human-readable report from the existing evidence-backed outputs.

Scope distinction: the primary bank-statement investigation covers `primary/` and `Secondary/`, matching the 162 processed source documents in the prior artifacts. The repository also contains `synthetic_data/`, which is a separate generated benchmark corpus with its own labelled CSV ground truth; it is documented in a dedicated section and not merged into the real-statement account, entity, or money-flow counts.

## Dataset Overview

- Statement/source documents processed: 162
- Raw extracted transactions before dedupe: 205,455
- Deduplicated investigation transactions: 183,192
- Account identifiers profiled: 111
- Banks identified in account profiles: 18 profile labels; summary list reports 17 identified banks plus Unknown where metadata was not reliable.
- Statement date range: 2009-06-12 to 2026-01-21
- Unsupported files in prior validation: []

| File type | Processed statement files |
| --- | ---: |
| PDF | 103 |
| XLSX | 23 |
| CSV | 11 |
| XLS | 22 |
| TXT | 3 |

Additional repository-supported files not merged into the real-statement corpus: 34 CSV files under `synthetic_data/` and the controlling prompt TXT. These were reviewed as supporting/generated benchmark materials, not as bank statements.

Metadata caution: several holder labels contain statement-header fragments rather than clean KYC names. Those labels remain as evidence-derived metadata, but behavioural findings rely on transaction evidence rather than inferred identity.

## Metadata Summary

- Extracted metadata fields include account holder/customer labels, bank, IFSC, currency, statement periods, account number, transaction dates, balances, references, counterparties, UPI IDs, and payment methods where present.
- Missing or unreliable metadata was retained as `Unknown` rather than filled by inference.
- Two UCO Bank PDF statements retained 439 transactions under account `UNKNOWN` because account-header extraction was distorted or masked; those transactions remain source-file and page linked.
- Duplicate statement formats were processed independently first and deduplicated globally for behavioural analysis. This is why raw extraction count is higher than the final transaction corpus.

Relationship interpretation: account-to-counterparty links are direct transaction relationships. Shared UPI, IFSC, bank, holder, and source-document links are metadata/entity relationships. No relationship is treated as personal control or common ownership unless the source evidence explicitly supports it.

### Cross-Account Relationship Checklist

| Requested relationship class | Evidence-backed result | Source artifact |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Linked accounts | Present through matched/observed money-flow records, account-holder metadata links, maintained-at-bank links, IFSC links, and repeated counterparty relationships. | `money_flow.json`, `relationship_dictionary.json` |
| Shared beneficiaries | Present as repeated transaction counterparties and repeated-beneficiary pattern instances. These are beneficiary/counterparty relationships, not assumed ownership relationships. | `relationship_dictionary.json`, `fraud_patterns.json` |
| Shared UPI IDs | Present: 478 `shared_upi_across_accounts` relationships and 10 shared-UPI pattern instances. | `relationship_dictionary.json`, `entity_dictionary.json`, `fraud_patterns.json` |
| Shared phone numbers | Phone-number entities were extracted, but the validated relationship dictionary did not promote a separate shared-phone-across-accounts relationship type. No unsupported shared-phone conclusion is asserted in this report. | `entity_dictionary.json` |
| Shared merchants | Present as repeated counterparty/merchant relationships and repeated beneficiary behaviour. Merchant identity is evidence-backed at transaction level; common ownership/control is not inferred. | `merchant_profiles.json`, `relationship_dictionary.json` |
| Shared IFSC codes | Present as 84 `uses_ifsc` relationships. IFSC reuse is treated as bank/branch-routing evidence, not proof of account linkage by itself. | `relationship_dictionary.json`, `ifsc_mapping_validation.json` |
| Shared branches | Branch-level metadata was not consistently promoted into a separate relationship type. Where branch evidence appears through IFSC or source metadata, it is retained as metadata rather than over-interpreted. | `source_inventory.json`, `entity_dictionary.json` |
| Money movement between accounts | Present as 5,114 money-flow records, including 74 high-confidence matched records and 5,040 medium-confidence observed or single-sided records. | `money_flow.json` |

## Transaction And Payment Behaviour

- Largest transaction amount observed: INR 17,764,659.71
- Smallest positive transaction amount observed: INR 0.01
- Average transaction amount: INR 11,333.85
- Most common payment method: UPI

Behaviour profile summary: the corpus is digitally intensive, with UPI dominating transaction count. RTGS, UPI, IMPS, NEFT, and Unknown categories dominate amount movement. Cash withdrawal behaviour remains significant and is repeatedly involved in post-credit cash-out patterns.

## Timeline Reconstruction

The chronological timeline in `timeline.json` and `timeline.md` contains 183,192 deduplicated events from 2009-06-12 through 2026-01-21. Early events include salary/ATM/POS activity from 2009-2011, while the heaviest suspicious-pattern concentration is in 2024-2026 statement activity.

Key timeline observations:
- Historical personal salary and ATM/POS behaviour exists in older statements, especially around account `38211367068923`.
- 2024-2026 files contain dense UPI, IMPS, NEFT, RTGS, cash-out, repeated-beneficiary, and high-velocity patterns.
- Duplicate PDF/XLS/XLSX/CSV representations of some statements were deduplicated for global analysis while retaining all source-document provenance.
- Two statements show no transaction activity and were retained as processed zero-activity evidence rather than ignored.

## Fraud Pattern Catalogue

The following catalogue is evidence-backed and conservative. Pattern labels are investigative indicators, not final fraud determinations. Each pattern instance maps to evidence IDs in `ground_truth.json` and to structured entries in `fraud_patterns.json`.

Risk categories were assigned from multiple evidence-backed indicators: transaction volume, digital intensity, cash-out ratio, rapid balance depletion, balance volatility, repeated counterparties, shared identifiers, reversal activity, and counterparty diversity. A single large transaction alone was not treated as sufficient for high risk.

## Synthetic Benchmark Files

The `synthetic_data/` folder is a labelled generated benchmark corpus rather than part of the real bank-statement investigation. It contains 33 generated case CSV files plus `synthetic_data/ground_truth/GROUND_TRUTH.csv`. Its summary reports 16,745 total transactions, 1,816 labelled fraud transactions, 14,929 innocent transactions, and 10.85% fraud prevalence.

| Synthetic pattern family | Files | Labelled fraud transactions | Evidence / signal |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| Round Trip | rt_case_01.csv, rt_case_02.csv, rt_case_03.csv | 75 | matched references, linked counterparties in narration, amounts, timing windows, and balance behaviour. |
| Transit / Layering | tl_case_01.csv, tl_case_02.csv, tl_case_03.csv | 99 | matched references, linked counterparties in narration, amounts, timing windows, and balance behaviour. |
| Accumulation | ac_case_01.csv, ac_case_02.csv, ac_case_03.csv | 124 | matched references, linked counterparties in narration, amounts, timing windows, and balance behaviour. |
| Structuring / Smurfing | st_case_01.csv, st_case_02.csv, st_case_03.csv, st_case_04.csv | 77 | matched references, linked counterparties in narration, amounts, timing windows, and balance behaviour. |
| Burst Activity | ba_case_01.csv, ba_case_02.csv, ba_case_03.csv | 546 | matched references, linked counterparties in narration, amounts, timing windows, and balance behaviour. |
| Duplicate Transactions | dup_case_01.csv, dup_case_02.csv, dup_case_03.csv | 74 | matched references, linked counterparties in narration, amounts, timing windows, and balance behaviour. |
| Money Trail | mt_case_01.csv, mt_case_02.csv, mt_case_03.csv, mt_case_04.csv | 56 | matched references, linked counterparties in narration, amounts, timing windows, and balance behaviour. |
| Aggregation | agg_case_01.csv, agg_case_02.csv, agg_case_03.csv | 392 | matched references, linked counterparties in narration, amounts, timing windows, and balance behaviour. |
| Circular Flow | cf_case_01.csv, cf_case_02.csv, cf_case_03.csv | 75 | matched references, linked counterparties in narration, amounts, timing windows, and balance behaviour. |
| Combined Patterns | combo_case_01.csv, combo_case_02.csv, combo_case_03.csv, combo_case_04.csv | 298 | matched references, linked counterparties in narration, amounts, timing windows, and balance behaviour. |

Synthetic files are useful for validating known pattern detection such as round trips, transit/layering, accumulation, structuring/smurfing, burst activity, duplicate transactions, money trails, aggregation, circular flow, and combined patterns. They should not be combined with the 162 real statement files when reporting case-bank accounts or risks.

## Confidence Levels

- High confidence: direct transaction evidence or repeated identifier evidence exists, source files are known, and the same behaviour is supported by multiple transactions or metadata records.
- Medium confidence: the transaction is observed and source-linked, but the opposing leg, complete account identity, or broader context is incomplete.
- Low confidence: not used in the final pattern catalogue; ambiguous observations were either retained as Unknown metadata or documented as limitations.

Pattern confidence distribution:

| Confidence | Pattern instances |
| --- | ---: |
| Medium | 276 |
| High | 183 |

## Evidence Framework

Every transaction-level conclusion cites evidence IDs of the form `EV_*`. Each evidence record in `ground_truth.json` contains source file, page/source location, transaction ID, account, transaction date, reference, amount, direction, narration, and confidence. The complete evidence table contains 183,192 records.

## Limitations And Remaining Uncertainties

- PDF processing used embedded text extraction; no OCR was performed.
- Some PDF-derived line parsing can be less reliable than tabular CSV/XLS/XLSX sources; those transactions generally carry medium confidence.
- Some source documents are duplicate representations of the same statement. They were processed, then deduplicated for global behavioural analysis.
- Single-sided large transfers are treated as observed movements, not confirmed internal flows, unless a corresponding transaction is matched by available evidence.
- Residual account ambiguity remains for transactions assigned to `UNKNOWN`; these are not discarded, but identity-based conclusions are limited.
- Modified case parameters may mean names, account numbers, or other sensitive fields are synthetic/masked while behavioural traces remain meaningful.

## Final Validation Report

| Validation item | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Total real statement/source files analyzed | 162 |
| Total supported files found in repository | 197 including synthetic benchmark CSVs and prompt TXT |
| Total accounts in real-statement corpus | 111 |
| Total entities | 71,186 |
| Total relationships | 56,490 |
| Total behavioural/fraud-related pattern instances | 459 |
| Total money-flow records reconstructed/observed | 5,114 |
| High-confidence money-flow records | 74 |
| Unsupported files | None reported in prior validation |
| Zero-transaction processed statements | 2 |
| Synthetic benchmark CSV files reviewed separately | 34 |
| Remaining uncertainties | Parser quality for some PDFs; unknown/distorted account headers; single-sided external flows |

Validation result: pass with limitations. The previous structured artifacts are substantially complete for `primary/` and `Secondary/`; the main gap was the short Markdown ground truth. This report fills that gap and documents the separate synthetic benchmark corpus without fabricating relationships across it.
